## Astorians

The Astorians were the dominant thieves' guild in the city of Teziir as of 1359 DR.

Activities
Their primary activities revolved around petty theft protection services, with goods often being offered as "tithes." The Council of Teziir wished to diminish the Astorians' influence within the city, but they simply could not afford the services required to do so effectively.

Members
The Astorians numbered nearly 700 members in 1359 DR and they were growing rapidly. Many believed the Astorians would end up ruling Teziir within a generation or two.

## Aspen

Aspens were a type of deciduous tree in the Realms.

Habitats
 In Maztica, aspens grew in the mountainous forests of the Pasocada Basin.
 In the Moonshae Isles, aspens grew in the forests of its lowlands, including the Dernall Forest and the Myrloch Vale.
 In interior Faerûn, aspens grew throughout the lowlands of the Cormyr countryside.
 In northwest Faerûn, aspens grew in the hills of Hartsvale.
 In the Hordelands, aspens were one of the three primary varieties of tree found in the southern forest of Shalhoond.
 In the Unapproachable East, aspens could be found within the Ashenwood.
 Beyond the Prime Material plane, aspens could be found in the valleys of the demiplane known as Barovia.

Usages
 Aspens were one of a handful of tree species whose bark and twigs were prized by giant beavers.
 Some wands of enemy detection were carved from the wood of an aspen.

Rumors & Legends
Much like the Pasocada Basin that they inhabited, the Azuposi believed that aspens could be found in the forests of their afterlife.

Notable Aspens
 Red Tree, a legendary large aspen in Immil Vale that had the spirits of two Witches of Rashemen tied to it.

## Asparagus

Asparagus, otherwise known as greenspears or greenneedles, were a type of vegetable found in the Realms.

Availability
Places in the Realms where asparagus was known to be produced were as follows:
 West of the Trade Way, the village of Cragmyr cultivated asparagus that they sold to merchants traveling to either Amn or Baldur's Gate.
 In west Faerûn, asparagus was cultivated in the village of Anga Vled and the city-state of Cursrah.
Greenspears were among the staples grown and consumed in virtually every household in Turmish, where it was steamed or pooled and served in butter. Older and harder asparagus was tossed in stews and soups.

Notable Asparagus Products
 Some used juiced asparagus or the roots of the asparagus plant in the making of sedatives or the treatment of heart problems.
 Oxtail soup was served in Sembia alongside pickled asparagus in a spicy Turmish cream sauce.
 Asparagus was served on the side with steak medallions – a dish for the nobility of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

Notable Users of Asparagus
Businesses
 Felzoun's Folly tavern in Waterdeep served bite sized portions of sausage rolls or chicken-and-cheese pastry with a side of fresh asparagus with melted butter.
 Morgan's Inn would often serve asparagus, mushrooms, and fried yellow peppers together in a broth of cream over a lightly smoked silverfin.
 The Old Troll's Foot in Kirinwood offered a spicy asparagus-and-artichoke bean broth. And The Old Oyster in Monksblade had a chopped roast almonds and asparagus broth on its menu.
 The Old Wheel served a dressing of groundspears juice on cold mixed greens.
 The Worried Wyvern inn served omelets with asparagus and spiced river fish.

Races & Sentient Creatures
 Asparagus was a staple in the diet of many elves, who were known to eat them both raw and steamed with herbs.

## Asborn Moonstar

Asborn Moonstar was a young and desperate nobleman from Waterdeep, member of the Moonstar family in the mid–14 century DR.

History
In early 1370 DR, Asborn Moonstar and Elene Ruldegost fell mindlessly in love with each other, despite their families' old animosity. When the youths decided to tie the knot, both families were against even the mere idea of that union, but the Ruldegosts agreed to permit the marriage if Asborn were to retrieve a crystal rose from Undermountain. These roses grew in one place, the Garden of the Drow, thought long abandoned. Asborn ran straight to the Yawning Portal and plunged into the dungeon.

The young Moonstar had been missing for over a week when Khelben Arunsun asked his trusted agent to find the youth in the abandoned drow monastic retreat. When Khelbenite made his way to the cave of the Garden. It became evident that the dark residents of the retreat were back, and it did not bode well for Asborn. Subsequently, the adventurer came across a sealed room with serene-looking springs, plants, mushrooms, and a single crystal rose growing in the center. Next to the delicate flower, the hero found the tattered remains of Asborn Moonstar. He was able to sneak past the drow into the rose chamber but was discovered and sealed inside, and the young man starved to death. There was nothing else to be done; Khelbenite collected the crystal flower and delivered it to Elene Ruldegost so she would have something by which to remember her departed lover.

## Asheru

Asheru was a powerful wizard and a senator of Innarlith in the mid–14 century DR.

Description
In the 1370s DR, he was middle-aged and he had long black hair which may have been dyed to mask the grey. He had gray-green eyes.

Personality
He was laconic.

Relationships
He was an ally on the senate with Senator Meykhati.

History
Sometime prior to 1340 DR, Asheru was responsible for making magical defenses to protect Pristal Towers in Innarlith, the private home of the fire genasi Pristoleph, one of the wealthiest people in the city.

In the 1360s DR, Asheru was both a senator and the head of an underground college of wizards.

Asheru joined in the funeral procession for the assassinated Senator Khonsu on Greengrass, 1364 DR. He, Meykhati, and other senators retired to the Peacock Resplendent inn after the funeral to speak with Willem Korvan, who had secretly assassinated Khonsu, in order to offer him a senate seat in exchange for him agreeing to vote for their causes on the senate for five years.

After Salatis became the new Ransar of Innarlith in 1365 DR, with the help of the Red Wizards he allowed for the establishment of a Thayan enclave in the city. Asheru, despite not being Thayan, abandoned his former position as head of the college and aligned himself with the enclave, in exchange for a few new spells and help with getting on some senate committees that he wanted to be appointed to.

In 1374 DR, during the outbreak of violence that took place between the faction led by the senate and the faction led by Ransar Pristoleph, Asheru sided with the senate and helped them in their efforts to unseat Pristoleph. When Pristoleph appeared defeated and the senate faction had seemingly gained control of the city, Asheru publicly suggested Marek Rymüt, the leader of the Thayan enclave, be appointed as the new Ransar of Innarlith. Marek, however, turned down the suggestion and said he would always offer his services to the city of Innarlith.

Pristoleph, however, triumphed in the struggle and Asheru was captured by Pristoleph's forces. Pristoleph had Marek, Asheru, and another mage named Kurtsson gagged and brought to the Palace of Many Spires to be judged. Pristoleph sentenced the three of them to be handed over to the Thayan authorities to be punished. He was sent into exile.

## Ash mephit

Ash mephits were a variety of mephit composed of ash.

Description
These mephits had powdery gray skin and lacked external ears. Whenever they spoke, it came out in a nasally whining tone.

Personality
Ash mephits had a perpetually depressed and gloomy disposition. They often subjected people to near-endless tales of their sorrows, boredom, and frustrations.

Abilities
Ash mephits were capable of spraying a cloud of choking ash in a  radius. They could gate in another of their kind once per hour. And they had an innate ability to, once per day, rally a group of good and neutrally aligned creatures into a distracting conversation, similar to the spell Leomund's lamentable belaborment.

They were immune to all forms of poison. They were impervious to harm from spells and abilities based around fire, heat, and cold. They were especially vulnerable to spells and abilities based around liquids or wind. When it came to weapons, they could only be harmed by slashing and piercing weapons that were enchanted.

Combat
Unlike some types of mephit, this variety was incapable of fighting by means of biting or clawing.

Society
Ash mephits were largely solitary creatures, though on their home plane, they lived in scattered communities. Such societies were little more than war-like hunting packs.

Relationships
These mephits regularly preyed upon the rasts of their home plane, who in turn preyed upon them.

Ash mephits sometimes acted as servants of the deity Laduguer.

## Assault on Myth Nantar (module)

Assault on Myth Nantar is an epic adventure module set in the Forgotten Realms using the Dungeons & Dragons 5-edition ruleset. It is part of the Adventurers League/Dreams of the Red Wizards series of adventures published for the D&D Adventurers League. It is an epic adventure involving multiple parties of adventures, centered around an attempt by the Red Wizards of Thay to conquer the fabled city of Myth Nantar. It is the third part of the Sea of Fallen Stars series.

## Ashtyrranthor

Ashtyrranthor was a red dragon ally of the githyanki in Stardock in the early 1490s DR.

History
The githyanki knight Jerath once saved Ashtyrranthor's life when she was a wyrmling.

Sometime in the late 15 century DR, Al'chaia led a group of githyanki into the Crystal Labyrinth while tracking down a colony of mind flayers in the level of Seadeeps. Upon discovery of the portal leading to Stardock, they converted the abandoned asteroid into a crèche for the breeding and training of young githyanki, which they named Crèche K'liir. The labyrinth was converted into a fortress from where they led attacks against the mind flayers and protected access to Stardock. Ashtyrranthor was the main red dragon of the group.

Activities
Ashtyrranthor usually patrolled outside Stardock or checked on the githyanki nursery.

Relationships
Ashtyrranthor was the mother of Infernexus, Ashranthax, Blazutranc, Meteoranzym, Smoakcant, and Zolcharrx. She was a friend of the blind knight Jerath.

## Astar's ring

Astar's ring was the enchanted wedding ring given to Lana Graysteel by Astar Graysteel in the mid-to-late-14 century DR.

Description
The beautiful wedding ring had the name "Astar" engraved on the inside of the band.

Powers
Astar's ring was enchanted for the use among shady and double-dealing individuals of Mulmaster. The ring kept its wearer safe from mind-affecting magics, fear, and poisons.

History
Astar's ring belonged to Astar Graysteel, one of the Blades of Mulmaster in the mid-14 century DR. His life was filled with dangers and double-crossings in service of Selfaril Uoumdolphin. Astar met Lana Graysteel, and they quickly fell in love and were wed with Astar's ring given to the young bride as a wedding band. Years later, Astar was betrayed by Selfaril and was denounced as a criminal. Astar Graysteel was forced to flee Mulmaster, and to protect his wife's life, he left without explanation. Several years later, Lana Graysteel was still grieving for her lost love. After Astar Graysteel's death at the tentacles of the malaugrym who wore the face of Eremuth Silverwind, the dead Blade's comrades traveled to Mulmaster and gave the grieving woman Astar's letter that revealed the truth about his departure. Saddened but relieved, she gave up Astar's ring as a way of turning a new page in her life.

## Aster's Edge

Aster's Edge was an enchanted longsword associated with the Order of the Aster that was said to have bestowed upon holy warriors by a being from the Upper Planes.

The sword personified the tender growth cultivated by Lathanders radiance and the fury possessed by his righteous followers. While the blade of Aster's Edge was etched with engravings of aster blossoms, it would also burst into a blue flame when drawn, emitting a silvery-blue radiance.

Powers
Aster's Edge possessed a powerful +3 enchantment and was especially deadly when wielded against fiendish creatures. It provided its wielder some protection from magical energy and spells.

While the exact origins of Aster's Edge were unknown, it was believed to have been handed down to a member of the order from some unknown angelic being. Legend state it was offered as reward for destroying a particularly powerful fiend, which was speculated to have been a balor.

## Aswal al-Mutiq

Aswal al-MutiqAn alternate spelling or typo is shown as "Aswar" in the Land of Fate source. was Caliph of In'aash when the Law of the Loregiver spread throughout Zakhara, the Land of Fate.

History
Aswal was aware that many of the neighboring city-states were toppled by the people when their rulers refused to accept the Law of the Loregiver and enlightenment. Deeply troubled by this, he invited the entourage of warriors and mystics who brought the word of enlightenment into his city to a private audience. He met with these messengers of Fate for five days and five nights, and then deliberated with himself for another five days and five nights before finally accepting the Law of the Loregiver into his city. Upon his acceptance, Aswal stepped down from his throne and helped the messengers spread the word of the Loregiver throughout Zakhara.

Aswal was succeeded by his oldest child who renamed In'aash to mark the beginning of a new era of enlightenment. The new city was called Muluk.

## Assume undead form

Assume undead form was an arcane and divine spell used by necromancers and priests devoted to Velsharoon.

Effects
With this spell the caster could temporarily transform themselves into an undead creature with which they were familiar. While "undead" the caster had the full range of the selected creature's abilities with the exception that victims slain by the undead form would not rise as undead. The undead form also held all of the inherent weaknesses of that particular form. Hence, a caster assuming the form of a vampire would be vulnerable to sunlight. The caster was unable to cast any of their memorized spells or use any of their special abilities while in undead form.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required as material components a small piece of bone or flesh from the desired undead body to cast.

## Astaroth's augmentation

Astaroth's augmentation was a divine alteration spell used only by specialty priests devoted to Gargauth called Malefactors.

Effects
Malefactors used this spell to permanently enhance their attributes by calling on Gargauth's divine power. Gargauth extracted a heavy price in exchange for this power, demanding the caster corrupt an individual in power or sacrifice a helpless sentient creature every three months in his name. If the caster ever broke this contract then Gargauth would not only remove the bestowed power for the appropriate attribute, but would proceed to weaken the caster in the same attribute.

The caster could enter into multiple pacts with Gargauth by casting this spell over and over again, but nothing short of a wish or divine intervention could separate the caster from the pact without incurring Gargauth's wrath.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required as material components three yellow feathers, a ruby worth at least 25 gp and the priest's holy symbol to cast.

## Aslyferund elven chain

Aslyferund elven chain were sets of enchanted elven chainmail crafted by Aslyferund of Myth Drannor.

Description
These chain mails, suits of finest mesh, were soft and light, weighting and feeling like regular clothes rather than heavy metal links.

Powers
These masterfully-crafted suits of chainmail were enhanced with +3 enchantment and allowed for the armor to soak part of the damage, further protecting the wearer. Due to the creation process of the Aslyferund elven chains, they could only we worn by creatures of elven blood.

History
These enchanted items were crafted by Aslyferund, one of the most famous armorers of Myth Drannor, before the city's fall in the Weeping War of 714 DR. Many sages entertained rumors that the more powerful sets of Aslyferund elven chain were created with the aid of a gold dragon.

By late 14 century DR, some of these sets of armor could be found in the Sword Coast North region, as well as in the town of Elventree of the Moonsea. By that same time, the imp Cespenar that once served the god Bhaal had learned Aslyferund's secret and could replicate it using a suit of bladesinger chain and a spell scroll of protection from normal weapons.

## Astral construct

Astral constructs were ephemeral creations of psions, built through the use of a psionic power of the same name. They served their creator for a short time before vanishing.

Description
Astral constructs did not have a unified appearance: they were sculpted by the psion's mind during the manifestation of the power used to create them. However, their appearance could not hide the ectoplasm used to create them: they were milky white, and often translucent. Their size was more or less the same between constructs of similar power.

Combat
The abilities of an astral construct were also selected during the manifestation by the creator. Lesser constructs, for instance, might be possessed of some resistance to energy damage, the ability to fly at a modest speed, being swift swimmers, or adept at certain combat maneuvers. Greater constructs had more options for their abilities, including the ability to deal energy damage, to attack with poison, superior defensive reinforcements, more complex combat maneuvers, and some even had the ability to progressively heal for their duration. Those constructs built at or near the apex of a psion's power were capable of psionic powers of their own, attacking with energy rays, teleporting short distances, blindsight, or even being naturally invisible.

History
An astral construct attacked Marrec's party in their passage through the Celestial Nadir during the spring of the . It resembled a human-sized wasp. No manifester was ever identified; Ususi Manaallin suspected that it may have been naturally spawned in the Nadir.

## Asharak

Asharak was a tiefling of Elturel that was forced from his home city along with others of his kind in the late 15 century DR.

He possessed some prowess in martial combat and readily shared his knowledge with others.

Relationships
Should anything have happened to Zevlor their group's journey, Asharak was set to take over as leader of the Elturian tieflings.

History
In the, Elturel was pulled down to Avernus in the Nine Hells and all its tiefling residents were expelled from the city upon its return to Faerûn. Asharak was among the group led by Zevlor, which traveled west for Baldur's Gate, but was forced to take shelter from goblin raids at the Emerald Grove near the River Chionthar.

Asharak spent some of his time training the tiefling community's children, such as Gan, Umi, and Ide, in basic combat skills. He beckoned local adventurers, like Wyll the Blade of Frontiers to help bolster the children's spirits and give them a fighting chance on the harsh road ahead of them.

## Aspenheight Gap

The Aspenheight Gap was a low mountain pass located on the island of Gwynneth within the Moonshae Isles.

Geography
The pass separated the Aspenheight range in the north from the Highlands in the south. It was approximately  long and connected the central Myrloch Vale with the Shrouded Lands to the west. It lay six days' ride north of Caer Corwell, and provided an easy if roundabout route from Corwell into Myrloch Vale.

The pass entered Myrloch Vale to the north of the Myrloch. This entrance was marked by a ring of stones that served as a natural windbreak and thus a perfect campsite.

## Asple

Asple was a small fishing community on the north-western shores of the Vilhon Reach.

Geography
Asple was nestled on the east most edge of the Aphrunn Mountains, on the shores of the Vilhon Reach and to the south of the island of Ilighôn.

Trade
Even though Asple's main production was fish, the town also housed a rich and prosperous stone quarry that supplied the Reach. The town of Asple also attracted industrious adventurers in search of old sunken ships flush with treasures as the local fishers were quite knowable on the matter.

History
Asple was settled on the north-western shores of the Vilhon Reach in 50 DR, during the period of fast expansion and settlement of the region.

As of the late 14 century DR, Asple remain an independent city-state, mostly due to lack of attention from its neighbors. However, the town posed little danger and was an easy target for a takeover.

## As'arem

As'arem, literally meaning Empire of Arem, was the nation of shalarin in Serôs beneath the Sea of Fallen Stars.

Geography
As'arem spanned much of the northeastern Sea of Fallen Stars, reaching from the Hmur Plateau and abyssal plains east of the Pirate Isles and north of the Whamite Isles, to the Caverns of Khinas and shores of the Great Dale in the Easting Reach. It was the largest nation beneath the Sea of Fallen Stars upon its founding and through at least the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

The empire comprised four kingdoms: the breadbasket of Es'daan on the Po'ioa Plateau south of Impiltur, the military powerhouse of Es'purr between the Bymmal Trench and the Paolin Reef west of Thesk, the rich and opulent religious center of Es'rath east of the Pirate Isles, and the art and smithing center of Es'roch in the Easting Reach.

Government
The empire was organized as a feudal system with an eadar (emperor) at the top. The eadar was sheltered and distant from the day-to-day affairs of the empire, and thus each of the four kingdoms were controlled directly by its own finar (king), who in turn were served by the golmars (dukes). It was sometimes said that As'arem was thus not one realm, but five realms (the four kingdoms and As'arem itself). While the eadar was the nominal ruler of this confederation of kingdoms, they shared power with the finars and their commands were not absolute.

The capital city was Us'rath, located within the kingdom of Es'rath, where the Great Palace of Ri'ta'panal was located. Each of the other three kingdoms' capitals also contained an imperial palace adjacent to the royal palace.

In the mid-to-late 14 century DR, As'arem had been ruled for over a century by its 22nd eadar, the then elderly and sickly Ri'ola'con. His heir was Ri'ola'kirk, a young ranger.

The empire held a seat at the Nantarn Council alongside the other notable nations of Serôs.

Trade
Es'daan was the center of agriculture in the empire, harvesting 80% of all of As'arem's food from their abundant fields of kelp, sponge, and sea urchin. They were also a major exporter, and provided approximately a quarter of Myth Nantar's food and similar amounts to other allies of the shalarins. Es'purr also had a sizeable amount of farmland, although that kingdom was perhaps better known for the hunting in its wildernesses.

Es'roch was the center of crafting, producing artistically carved coral and jewelry, as well as weapons, armor (including the shalarins' famous silverweave and pearl armors), and other metalwork. They used geothermal heat to smelt and work metals.

Culture
As'arem was a highly structured and traditionalist society with a strict caste system consisting of the Seekers (which included the priests of the Faith caste, mostly located in Es'rath), the Scholars, the Providers (which included both Servants and Rulers), and the Protectors (the soldiers and hunters). The Protectors were mostly located in Es'purr, and their equivalents in Es'rath were sometimes called the Warriors. While military prowess was central to the culture of much of As'arem, the empire dogmatically did not aspire to expand, considering their current borders to be dictated by destiny. As a result, they were zealously protective of their territory.

Each of the four kingdoms could be said to have its own unique subculture: Es'daan were more laid back and less interested in politics; Es'purr was the most militant as well as the historic center for arcane magical study, having produced the largest number of shalarin Dukars; Es'rath was opulent and traditional, and was the center of religion in the empire; and Es'roch was the most industrious of the kingdoms as well as the center of shalarin art and music.

As'arem used the Serôsian calendar.

Architecture
Buildings in As'arem were often square and made from hydrozoan wood reinforced with kelp and pearl. Roofs were tiled with seashells. In the cities, coral and stone were also common construction materials, with brilliant white star coral used exclusively to build the four imperial palaces. Domes and towers were common motifs on more extravagant buildings.

At great depths, As'arem settlements were often lit by lucent coral.

Religion
The city of Us'rath in Es'rath was where the highest concentration of shalarin temples was located, owing to that city being where the Wildtide Gate was located. The empire venerated Faerûnian gods, but ascribed different names and some altered aspects in accordance with their ancient religion and culture. The most important deity and their "mother goddess" was Trishina (Ri'daa'trisha), followed in importance by Eldath (Ri'daa'lada), Persana (Ri'dea'prasa), Istishia (Ri'dea'shia), Mystra (Ri'dea'mystra), and others. They honored different deities as the patrons of different castes. In the mid-to-late 14 century DR, worship of Deep Sashelas (Ri'dea'shela) was becoming increasingly popular.

Following the Fifth Shalarin Passing, some shalarin of As'arem returned to the worship of their silent ancestral gods, and in particular turned away from Umberlee (Ri'dea'brela) to do so. At the same time, a cult of Dagon known as the Cult of Corynactis began to take root in the empire.

Defenses
The As'arem military was outfitted largely by troops from Es'purr, who defended not only their own borders but those of Es'daan and Es'roch as well. Their standing army was known as the Knights of Es'purr and comprised not only warriors but rangers and wizards. The kingdom maintained a Military Academy at Us'curn.

Over the course of its existence, As'arem had been both allies and enemies with nations of nearly every other race in Serôs—including the merfolk, sea elves, ixitxachitls, merrow, koalinth, sahuagin, and tritons—often existing at best in a state of tense neutrality. However, as of the, they had joined with many of these races as part of the Nantarn Alliance in an effort to create a stable peace. Owing to the closer ties to the tritons that this alliance afforded, the As'arem military gained access to the tapal in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

History
While the term "As'arem" may have been used to refer to the shalarin lands in Serôs much earlier, the realm itself was formed in the, shortly after the Third Shalarin Passing, during which the kingdoms of Es'daan and Es'purr were founded by newly arrivedf shalarin from the Sea of Corynactis. These fledgling shalarin kingdoms united with the more established shalarin kingdom of Es'rath to form the As'arem empire under the first eadar, Ri'ara'tup.

As'arem fought alongside Hmurrath in the Eighth Serôs War (–) against the ixitxachitls of Paolan Reef.

Following the end of the Ninth Serôs War (–), As'arem worked with the merfolk and Dukars of Serôs in hopes of creating a treaty to prevent future genocidal conflicts. In the, they were party to the drafting of The Laws of Battle.

During the Tenth Serôs War (–), As'arem found themselves at war simultaneously with the merfolk and sea elves.

In the, following the Fourth Shalarin Passing, Es'roch was founded by the latest wave of newcomers from the Sea of Corynactis as the fourth kingdom of As'arem, and the empire achieved the geographic size that it would maintain for the next several centuries, save for a brief period of expansion of Es'purr under the rule of its 60th finar, Ri'ala'tarl, between the, and the .

During the Eleventh Serôs War (–), As'arem allied with the merrow, koalinth, and merfolk peoples against the sea elves of Keryvyr, Naramyr, and Selu'Maraar. This was during the reign of the 17th eadar, Ri'ara'kos.

In the, Ri'ola'con became the 22nd eadar at age 116.

In the, an assassin cut short the 78 year rule of Es'purr's 61st finar, Ri'ala'porath, and the Es'purr royal seat was usurped by Ri'yra'krin IX. The usurper's rule lasted one month before he was executed by the Es'purr military, and the rightful heir, Ri'ala'sord, became the 62nd finar of Es'purr. However, this began an 80 year feud between the ruling Ala clan and the usurper Yra clan, who had been the kingdom's historic rulers. Both Ri'ala'sord and her heir, Ri'ala'tarl II, would be assassinated. Finally, Ri'ala'vor, the 64th finar of Es'purr, ended the feud by decimating the Yra clan and exiling the survivors.

During the Time of Troubles in the, aggressive magical currents appeared in As'arem, and strangled Finar Ri'ala'vor.

The shalarin did not at first participate in the Twelfth Serôs War in the, however they did join an alliance with the merfolk of Voalidru and the tritons of Pumana amid concern about the intentions of the evil armies of Iakhovas. Then, on Eleint 2, they entered the war in response to an attack by Iakhovas's sahuagin on three hatcheries in Es'rath which resulted in the deaths of over one thousand unhatched shalarins. Also lost in this attack was Ri'ola'sur, the heir of Eadar Ri'ola'con. Still grieving, Ri'ola'con named Ri'ola'kirk as his new heir on Highharvestide, and joined the Nantarn Alliance alongside Myth Nantar and Xadea. By the war's end, the finar of Es'roch, Ri'ena'plir, would also be slain while banishing Iakhovas from Serôs.

After the end of the war on Uktar 12 of 1369 DR, Eadar Ri'ola'con made a surprise move by taking up residence in the Palace of Ienaron in Myth Nantar without consulting the finars. All four finars became increasingly concerned that Ri'ola'con intended to tie the shalarian empire closer to the other peoples of Serôs, which the four were unprepared to do on account of the long history of struggle the shalarin had faced. There were whispers that some plotted to leave the As'arem confederation or even to remove the eadar. Indeed, Ri'ola'con would be assassinated by poison on Greengrass of the, leading to political fracturing in As'arem between the Warrior caste of Es'rath and their rivals in Es'roch.While the Sea of Fallen Stars sourcebook (p. 75) states that Ri'ola'con was assassinated in 1370 DR, the novel Queen of the Depths features Ri'ola'con alive and still serving in his capacity as eadar as of 1373 DR.

Following the Fifth Shalarin Passing in the, the Wildtide Gate did not close as it normally did, leaving As'arem linked to the Dagon-worshiping shalarin civilization in the Sea of Corynactis. Keeping the open portal a secret while also combating the rise of the Cult of Corynactis within their own realm became the new priority.

In the, As'arem suffered from dragon attacks owing to the Rage of Dragons that year, and joined their allies to face a flight of dragons at Myth Nantar. In exchange for securing powerful weapons for this battle from a shalarin priestess of Umberlee, Tu'ala'keth, the eadar was forced to swear that all of As'arem would pay homage at Umberlee's temples once each tenday through the .

Notable Locations
Us'daan, capital of the kingdom of Es'daan.
Us'purr, capital of the kingdom of Es'purr.
Us'rath, capital of the kingdom of Es'rath and of the whole empire.
Us'roch, capital of the kingdom of Es'roch.

Notable Inhabitants
Ri'ola'con, the 22nd eadar of As'arem, and ruled the empire from the late–11 century DR through the mid-to-late 14 century DR. He was assassinated in 1370 DR.
Ri'ola'kirk, a young ranger who was named Ri'ola'con's heir in 1369 DR.
Ri'ioa'culn II, the 36th finar of Es'daan, and ruled from 1358 DR through at least 1370 DR.
Ri'ala'tad, the 65th finar of Es'purr, and ruled from 1358 DR through at least 1370 DR.
Ri'yea'var, the 32nd finar of Es'rath, and ruled from  through at least 1370 DR.
Ri'ena'wyn, the 9th finar of Es'roch, and ruled from 1369 DR through at least 1370 DR.

## Assassination: The Complete Guidebook

Assassination: The Complete Guidebook was an assassination manual published several decades before 1372 DR.

History
As of the, a copy of Assassination: The Complete Guidebook was stashed on a bookshelf in Castle Exeltis's basement. The family's scion mismanaged the once-flourishing winery, and the bookshelves in the castle basement were covered in a thick layer of dust, evidence of a dire literary neglect. Unlike all other books there, the Assassination tome was clean and well-used. The shelf also hid a secret passage from the basement to the castle's second floor and the lord's chambers with the secret switch hidden behind the copy of the Assassination.

## Asrai

Asrai, also referred to as water sprites in the Beastlands and Sjöra on Ysgard, were shy, delicate and elusive faeries that lived in deep waters.

Description
Arai appeared as delicate, humanoid-shaped faeries that stood around  tall. They had with long golden hair that was constantly growing, often reaching down to their buttocks or calves. Asrai rarely ever wore clothing, preferring instead to use their hair to cover their modesty. Their hair was sort of a living extension of them, twisting and flexing in unconscious reaction to their feelings.

Personality
Asrai were highly intelligent creatures, but shy and fearful to the point of never speaking for fear of being tricked into captivity.

Abilities
Asrai had only a single magical ability of note: when a group of them swam in a weaving, darting water dance, they created the effects of hypnotic pattern. Notably, charmed fish would refuse to attack asrai.

Their bodies had a severe sensitivity to true sunlight, to the point that exposure could kill them. However, artificial sunlight like that of the spell sunray had no effect on them. Upon death, they dissolved into small pools of water. Some types of deep-dwelling asrai, primarily those that inhabited the River Oceanus, additionally could not survive in air for any length of time and would die if captured.

Combat
These faeries never attacked out of malice or vengeance, but only to defend themselves. They carried no weapons and instead used their magic and their teeth if needed. Though the asrai that inhabited Arborea were known to wield small bows made of springy willow and braided water, with arrows smeared in fish guts that could potentially give those hit by them a disease.

Society
Asrai lived as wanderers, traveling their watery homes in schools, much like fish. The leaders of these schools were referred to as "pilots", who guided their yearly navigation from warmer waters to cooler ones. Competition between asrai to become a pilot's apprentice could be quite intense.

Asrai also had a love for pearls and other shiny, lustrous valuables, but were too small to carry such items. Instead, a school of asrai would have one or two small items of great beauty (but not necessarily great value).

It was said that an asrai lived for only nine years, after which she dissolved into water that spontaneously spawned two to five new asrai that were akin to the "mother" in all respects.
Diet
The asrai were a vegetarian species. They fed on algae and water plants such as kelp, as well as food thrown on the surface of the water (for this behavior, they were sometimes mistaken for minnows). They did not, however, ever eat meat; nor did other animals of the waters prey upon them.
Homelands
Asrai made their homes deep beneath lakes, rivers, and seas. They were strictly nocturnal, coming up to feed only at night. They could be found in both fresh and salt water bodies, though typically lived in bodies of freshwater and would feel sluggish for a few days following a transition from one type of water to another. In bodies of freshwater they laired in shadowy places such as caves, logs, or under banks.

On the Outer Planes asrai could be found in bodies of water in the Beastlands, Ysgard, and Arborea. Those in Arborea acted as protectors of its many warriors. The largest school of them in the Outer Planes could be found in the River Oceanus, though they rarely surfaced, except on nights when the algae bloomed.

Asrai also had loose ties to the Seelie Court, though rarely showed themselves there.
Languages
In addition to speaking their own languages, asrai spoke the languages of balaena, fish, mermaids, nixies, sirines, tritons, and sea elvish. The asrai that inhabited Arborea additionally knew languages of various nature spirits, such as dryads and oreads.

Relationships
They were known to keep company with nymphs, sirines, selkies, and various aquatic animals. They were often welcomed by these creatures and sea elves within the Seelie Court. The asrai that inhabited Arborea often coordinated with the plane's nature spirits to carry out punishments on anyone that infringed Arborean waters. And the asrai of the River Oceanus sometimes acted as companions, guides, or translators for the balaena.

Because of their hypnotic pattern ability's tendency to make sailors fall into water, sharks, giant pikes, and various predatory species of fish sometimes followed asrai schools in hopes of preying upon such unlucky sailors. Though the fish rarely had any bond of friendship with the asrai themselves.

Some evil fisherfolk were known to set out bait for asrai and capture them inside of amphora enspelled with darkness for the purpose of selling to creatures of the Lower Planes that considered them to be delicacies, such as the hydroloths, marraenoloths, and slaadi. A captured asrai usually sold for around 200 to 300 gold pieces.

Asrai were known to act as servants of Deep Sashelas, Erevan Ilesere, and Hanali Celanil.

Rumors & Legends
According to ancient legends the asrai once served as guides across all of the River Oceanus, but had long since abandoned their duty. There also existed rumors of asrai possibly inhabiting the Elemental Plane of Water, the aqueducts and sewers of Sigil, and even the Prime Material plane. However, due to their elusiveness these rumors were unconfirmed.

Other rumors spoke of there being an asrai queen in the Seelie Court, hidden within either a bottomless well or a spring of pure, everflowing water.

## Asafetida

Asafetida, also known as gum asafetida, devil's dung, or blood of the gods, was a type of gum resin extracted from certain plants' taproots and rhizomes and that was dried and used as a spice or condiment.

Availability
Aurora's Emporium sold asafetida for 5 sp per, intended for wizards to use in spells and laboratory experiments.

Usages
Asafetida was believed to have some medicinal uses, and tonics brewed with the substance was said to be beneficial for one's brain and nervous system, as well as acting as stimulants and aphrodisiacs.

Novice alchemists learned how to prepare basic alchemical powders from common ingredients, with a powerful aphrodisiac made from asafetida, cayenne, and euphorbia. Another recipe for a general nervous disorder treatment included asafetida, lavender, coriander, and bryony.

Asafetida was one of the ingredients in the special enchanted ink used to scribe scrolls of shocking grasp. Asafetida, balm of gilead, ginseng, and mace (or masterwort), were burnt into ash, mixed with ash of a tree incinerated by lightning, and mixed with the rest of ingredients.

Stinkpots could be made with asafetida, alongside other volatile or pungent ingredients.

## Asmagh's Alley

Asmagh's Alley was a backalley that was located in the city of Waterdeep.

"Each ward has its own history, legends, and traditions based around who lived there in the past, famous or infamous events, and the uncanny things that continue to occur. For example, children (and even some adults) hop on one foot when crossing Asmagh's Alley in the Castle Ward. Why? Well, Asmagh was an apothecary who poisoned many patients, then buried them upright beneath the alley under cover of night. He was discovered, and some say that as many as eighty bodies were subsequently pulled up from holes under the alley's wide flagstones. Though this happened over a century ago, children passing through the alley still sing a song: “Hop for the hollows, hop for the dead, hop on the flagstones, hop on their heads.” As you stroll down Warrior's Way or the Street of Silver, listen for the children's delighted screams and go give it a try." -Volo's Waterdeep Enchiridion (Waterdeep: Dragon Heist)

Location
Asmagh's Alley was located in the central section of Waterdeep's Castle Ward. The alley ran north to south, originating on Selduth Street and ending in the south on Palfrey Lane. It ran parallel to Warrior's Way.

## Asavir

Asavir was an infamous legendary pirate from the Nelanther Isles and the namesake of Asavir's Channel, active in the early 11 century DR.

Activities
Asavir commanded a fleet of over a dozen vessels.

History
In the, Captain Asavir made history by capturing trade cargoes from twelve Amnian merchant families, claiming a season's worth of commodities from over one hundred ships. This feat was considered the most impressive pirate operation that had ever taken place along the Sword Coast.

Several years later, Asavir and his entire fleet were lost in a deadly storm west of the Nelanther Isles in the Sea of Swords.

Location
The villa was located in the central section of Waterdeep's Sea Ward on the northeast corner of where Diamond Street and the Street of the Singing Dolphin joined.

Description
The walled compound contained four separate buildings. The larger buildings stood on the south side of the estate. Each building stood between one and three stories tall.

In the 14 century DR, rumors circulated that the goddess Siamorphe had manifested in the villa during the Time of Troubles and protected both the North and Sea Wards from suffering large-scale damages at the hands of Myrkul's undead legions. Whilst initially shrugging it off, Lord Laeros Assumbar came to take pride in the notoriety that the rumor brought House Assumbar.

## Assume gaseous form

Assume gaseous form was a spell used by clerics of Aerdrie Faenya.

Effects
When cast, this spell transformed the caster, and their possessions, into a gaseous state. The transformation took several minutes, during which the caster could not move or do anything else, as the spell required great concentration and focus from the caster. They were also completely vulnerable during the transformation and the spell could not be cast if the caster was interrupted. Once successfully cast, the caster was no longer required to maintain concentration on the spell.

While they were in a gaseous state, the caster could move into places where mist could, and was protected from physical attacks. The caster could not speak and was vulnerable to wind currents and to fire. Once cast, the spell could not be reversed by the caster until it expired. Dispel magic could end the spell before it ran its course. However, such an act brought great pain and seizures to the caster. Healing magic such as cure light wounds could aid the caster while in this state.

Usage
While traditionally a spell granted by the elven goddess of the Seldarine Aerdrie Faenya, slayer genies of Zakhara were known to cast this spell.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required material components, the holy symbol of Aerdrie Faenya.

## Asaudrae Daerantree

Asaudrae Daerantree was a human priestess of Mielikki who lived in Amphail on the Sword Coast North in the 11 century DR. She was the origin of the annual Rite of the Stag Lass celebrated in that village.

Powers
Thanks to a gift of Mielikki, Asaudrae had a special power to change into the form of a stag. (Some scholars tried to correct this to a doe, the female deer, but were mistaken.)

Activities
She spent many of her days in stag form, exploring the nearby forest and learning about its flora and fauna.,

History
One day, Asaudrae was surprised by ignorant nobles from Waterdeep on a hunting trip who shot and wounded her with their crossbow bolts and javelins. She fled back to the village with the hunters in pursuit. Local folklore maintained that the hunters continued to assail her even after she reverted to her injured human form, but the nobles denied any such knowledge of her true nature. Asaudrae stumbled into the Stag-Horned Flagon, bleeding from quarrels in her flesh and begging for help, but died on the bar just as the hunters arrived with knives drawn.

Ever since that day, according to the locals, the Rite of the Stag Lass was performed annually in some form or other. Some villagers and most Mielikkians believed that Asaudrae was the Lady of the Forest herself and that their settlement and the Stag-Horned Flagon were sacred to her.

## Astral elf

Astral Elves were a variety of elves native to the Astral Plane. They traced their roots to the Feywild like other elves, but were very different from their terrestrial brethren. The millennia they spent within the Astral Plane imbued them with a spark of divine energy, bestowed upon them by divine powers.

Description
Because astral elves lived closer in proximity to their gods, they each had a notable starry gleam in their eyes.

Personality
Just as other living beings, astral elves did not age while within the Astral Plane. Their longevity―which often surpassed that of Torillian elves up to thousands of years―granted them unique outlooks on time and existence. This manifested in feelings of depression in some elves or apathy in others. Yet some kept away concerns over existential dread by exploring personal creativity, living introspective lives enriched by peaceful meditation, or set out to explore the far-off planes of the multiverse.

Society
Like other beings, astral elves traveled away from the Deep Astral in order to procreate and raise their children to a certain age.

Astral elves utilized spelljammers such as star moths―crafted from unique botanic and crystalline substances―to travel across both the Astral Plane and the far-off reaches of wildspace. It was there that they collected starlight, which they used to power and repair their vessels.

Abilities
Astral elves did not need to eat for sustenance, nor sleep for rest. Rather, they entered into a form of reverie referred to as an "astral trance".

They had especially acute senses, including darkvision that extended out to . One or more times each day, astral elves could teleport up to  away by magical means.

It was said that the leaders of the astral elves possessed the power to summon solar dragons to aid in their causes.

Possessions
In deference to the gods of the Seldarine, astral elves wore armor that featured ornate headpieces and visored helms that obscured their personal identity. They preferred to be seen as entirely faceless when dealing with members of other species.

The astral elves' gods imbued their weaponry with divine radiant energy that originated from starlight.

History
During some unknown era, groups of elves traveled away from their home in the Feywild to the Astral Plane, with the hope of becoming closer to their gods. They were said to be among the first creatures to take up permanent residence within the plane known to some as the Silver Void. Throughout their existence, astral elf civilizations have repeatedly defended themselves from horrific monstrosities and invasion forces, such as psurlons, illithids, and githyanki.

At some point, an entire fleet of astral elf vessels arrived at the Rock of Bral, warning the captains of the docked spelljammers against intervening upon their invasion of a single planet.

Notable Astral Elves
 Prince Xeleth, the would-be ruler of the Xaryxian Empire.

## Astar Graysteel

Astar Graysteel was a disgraced Blade of Mulmaster and an adventuring mercenary, active in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
At the time of his death, he was forty-odd years old. Astar was a grim-faced, tall, and muscular human male. In his later years, he had salt-and-pepper greying hair and icy-blue eyes that matched it. His body language and stature betrayed his formidable skills of a warrior. The man had a deep and confident voice.

Personality
Astar Graysteel was an honorable man who was forced to take a blade as a mercenary. He was a grim yet kind man who often fought for glory and to defend the vulnerable. As a mercenary, Astar learned to prepare for battle against a variety of opponents, to carry enchanted arms, and be stocked up on potions.

Possessions
Astar Graysteel was armed with an enchanted +2 longsword and a magic +2 shield. He had a cloak of protection draped over his muscular shoulders. He was armored in a set of enchanted half-plate. Astar used his enchanted ring as a wedding ring that he put on his beloved wife's finger.

History

Astar Graysteel was born in the city of Mulmaster on the Moonsea. Eventually, he became one of the Blades of Mulmaster, and stayed with the organization until the mid-14 century DR. His life was filled with dangers and double-crossings in service of the High Blade of Mulmaster – Selfaril Uoumdolphin. Astar built a family after meeting and swiftly falling in love with Lana Graysteel. She received Astar's magic ring as a wedding band. Years later, after betraying his master, Astar Graysteel was forced to flee Mulmaster. He was falsely denounced as a criminal, forcing Astar to leave his beloved wife behind. In order to save her life, Astar left her in the dark about the true reasons for his departure, leaving Lana heartbroken. In the years that followed, Graysteel fought for coin and fought for good across the Sword Coast, in the far North, and through the Western Heartlands with the Flaming Fist, the Swords of Tempus, and the Crimson Guard.

In the winter of the, strife and discord shook the trade routes of the Moonsea. A sinister new organization rose to terrorize the southern Moonsea region – the Order of the Crippled Fang. It was known for flooding the roads with fiends from lower planes. Several weeks after the Cult's attacks started, many communities of the Moonsea were in a state of panic, especially after an attack on Teshwave and constant presence along the Shind Road. Astar heard whispers of the Cult raising on the Moonsea. At the time, Astar was fulfilling his contract in Cormyr, wiping out a gang of murderous orcs. Once he collected the pay, the warrior headed to the city of Voonlar, where he roomed at the Swords Meet tavern for several days. One evening, the town was quaked and flooded with fiends of the Order. Astar Graysteel, along with city guards and several other adventurers, defended the settlement and saved lives.

The morning after the attack, Astar Graysteel met the Hero of Daggerford, who helmed the defense and killed several members of the Cult who attacked a temple in Voonlar. Joined by Harper Sharalyn Lockleaf, mage Eremuth Silverwind, and an undead rogue Lyressa, the group set out to stop the dreaded new threat to the Moonsea.

He kept his enchanted armor and wore it during his years of mercenary work away from home. He wore the half plate until his death at the tentacles of a malaugrym masquerading as his comrade Eremuth Silverwind in the . Honoring his dying wish, the Hero of Daggerford and Astar's companions traveled to Mulmaster and gave the grieving woman Astar's letter that revealed the truth about his departure. Saddened but relieved, she gave up Astar's ring as a way of turning a new page in her life.

## Assikhath

The Assikhath was a huge cavern-lair located under Thaymount.

Defenses
The cavern was guarded by several powerful statues, some of which could cast protective magic while others animated to defend the place against intruders. Most of those statues had snake heads and represented stylized Sarrukhs.

History
The Assikhath was created by the Ba'etith as a place to hold the Athora.

Rumors & Legends
Due to the statues guarding the cave, most Thayan considered snake-headed statues as a symbol of power and success in magic. Consequently such statues were a common sight in Thayan homes and enclaves, and were thought to bring good luck.

She was overweight and had a motherly air about her.

Activities
Aszerra lived in Ordulin in northern Sembia. She was one of only a handful of known priests of Shaundakul following the end of the Time of Troubles in the .

## Ash Ridge

The Ash Ridge was a mountainous area of the Sword Mountains, along the Thunderhoof Pass.

Geography
Ash Ridge stood to the northeast from Gnomengarde, long the mountain pass towards the Old Owl Well valley.

History
Before the 15 century DR, the Thunderhoof Pass was an important trade way that led to the Old Owl Well. Following the disastrous Spellplague that ravaged the face of Toril, the Thunderhoof Pass, the Ash Ridge, and the Old Owl Well were taken over by the orcs of the Many-Arrows Kingdom. Powers of the Lords' Alliance showed little interest in the mountain pass as the trade withered away. Despite having little strategic value, Lord Neverember used adventurers to claim the Thunderhoof Pass for Neverwinter, with a promise of grand rewards.

## Astralomitis

Astralomitis was a disease that affected astral dragons.

Acquisition
Considered rare and unusual, astralomitis was more common amongst astral dragons who switched between planes frequently.

Effects
The disease inhibited an astral dragon's ability to travel the planes via plane shift. If an astral dragon was on a plane other than the Astral Plane when struck by astralomitis, it was unable to return, and there was a chance that insanity would ensue. It lost the ability to cast any spells in this state and ferociously attacked any creatures nearby, with its madness lending it greater force, while its breath weapon functioned as normal. This state could last for two to five days, and resulted in the death of the dragon, with it collapsing and dying in less than four hours.

Treatment
If the infected dragon could somehow return to the Astral Plane before its death (such as via a portal or another's aid), the sickness and insanity slowly diminished, but it was left unable to switch planes. The disease had no known cure except for a wish spell.

## Asha the Silent

Asha the Silent was a wolf and later human who became High Moonmistress of Moonshadow Hall in Yhaunn during the 8 century DR. She was known for never speaking during her time at Moonshadow Hall.

History

Asha was originally the wolf Halftail, the animal companion of Niree Swifthands, the high priestess of the Temple of the Half-Moon in Elmwood. Niree was a lycanthrope and member of the New Moon Pact, and was executed when the Pact was declared heretical by the church of Selûne at the behest of High Moonmistress Brima Chintamn in the . Halftail avoided capture by the forces led by Bright Lady's Tower and was transformed into a human by the hand of Selûne, taking the name Asha. She was charged by the goddess with protecting the legacy of the New Moon Pact and safeguarding the Selûnite faith against threats from within and without.

Later in that year, she joined Moonshadow Hall as a priestess, and on the Feast of the Moon she began writing The Book of the New Moon, a tome that described the true nature of the New Moon Pact, as well as the sacred rite that allowed a lycanthrope to join the Pact and become empowered by Selûne. After completing the tome, she hid it in the archives of the temple so that it might be found in the future and be used to redeem the Pact. She eventually became the High Moonmistress of Moonshadow Hall despite never speaking a word.

Asha's hopes for the Pact's legacy were ultimately fulfilled in the, when the werewolf High Moonmistress Feena Archwood found the tome and recreated the Pact.

## Assembly

The Assembly was a large meeting hall in the town of Thunderstone in Cormyr in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Structure
The Assembly resembled an enormous barn, with a single large room.

Services
The Assembly was used to hold a variety of gatherings, including private, civic, and religious assemblies and events, and as a general meeting place. Locals and outsiders, especially adventurers, who were looking for companionship, gossip, or work gathered there, and "help wanted" notes and other notices were posted on the inside walls. In bad weather, it was used as an under-cover town square or marketplace, where vendors erected market stalls to sell food, drink, and other goods and trinkets. Occasionally, public bouts were hosted, and these were the subject of discreet betting.

## Askalet

Askalet was the easternmost of the three islands located south of Thelve and southwest from the the fortress library of Candlekeep. During the century that followed the Spellplague, its was a part of the so-called Cimarine Isles.

Description
Askalet was a rocky outcropping that rose above the Sea of Swords. The island of fissured granite with temperate woods growing among the cracks. Askalet had rock-basin pools of freshwater collected from melted snow and summer fogs as well as several freshwater springs. A small two-vessel harbor was located at the center of the island's southern shores. Not far from it stood a fish-filled freshwater pond at the center of a wooded alley – the source of a dozen freshwater springs that trickled down the slop, eventually emptying into the Sea of Swords. In winter, the spring and their miniature waterfalls froze into a delicate and beautiful ice wall. Beyond the wooded valley, Askalet was a collection of wooded cliffs and ridges.

Askalet's eastern side was  long, northern and western shores were  long each.

The island was once inhabited, but by the late 14 century DR, it only held at least three ruins: an overgrown cottage with its roof collapsed located in the westernmost dell on Askalet. A ruined stone estate stood in the valley not far from the harbor, rumored to hide a pirate's trapped treasure. Ruins of a castle rose above the sea atop the island's easternmost cliffs. The castle was falsely believed to had been a pirate-baron's keep and, at one point – a mage's tower. Elminster Aumar theorized that the said wizard, Askalath was the island's namesake.

Geography
Askalet's northern shores were separated by Skoond's Run from the large island of Thelve. To Askalet's west laid two sister islands of Nairn's Tomb and Arthoum. The isles of Askalet and Nairn's Tomb were  apart, while Arthoum laid  north of Askalet. The Sword Coast mainland was separated from Askaletby a  body of water at its narrowest and the strait known as the Racewind Passage.

Flora & Fauna
Askalet was home to a massive population of seabirds numbered in thousands. The island's rocks were stained with the birds' guano. Several species of predatory sea creatures could also be found on the island. Deer and an occasion owlbear called the wooded labyrinth of rocks and fissure-caverns their home.

History
Askalet was once used by pirates and smugglers who likely gave the island its name. By the late 14 century DR, the island's ruins were used by many as a shelter through the years. Despite that, the island was far from thoroughly looted. Askalath's tower had a well-hidden cellar with a shaft that dropped down into the dangerous depths of the Underdark. Some believed that the wizard used the shaft as a well, magically retrieving water from the far underground. However, the shaft's true value was in the multitude of cellar complexes, with their entrances opening into the endless shaft's lower reaches.

Appendix
References

## Askalath

Askalath was a mage who once resided on the isle of Askalet in the Sea of Swords, many centuries before the 14 century DR.

History
By the late 14 century DR, little remained recorded of Askalath's history. Elminster Aumar, the Sage of Shadowdale, believed that the mage's island, Askalet, got its name from a corruption of "Askalath." The mage resided in his tower, long a ruin in the 14 century DR. By that time, his home was looted of most of the treasures. However, in the tower's basement, one could find a long, seeming infinite well, a shaft that plummeted deep into the bowels of the Undermountain. Cellar complexes were hidden closer to the shaft's end, with entrances accessible from the bottom of the well.

## Askalath's tower

Askalath's tower was a ruined castle once owned by the mage Askalath on the isle of Askalet in Sea of Swords, just off the shores of Candlekeep.

Location
The castle was built on the island's easternmost cliffs' southern edge.

Structure
By the late 14 century DR, the tower castle was in ruins and mainly used as shelter by monsters and castaways. Most of its treasured had long been scavenged, but its stone-carved cellar hid secrets. A seemingly endless well shaft descended in a straight fall directly into the Underdark. It was unknown if the well was used for water by Askalath. If it was, it had to be done via magic. Somewhere in the lower parts of the shaft, one could access many short cellar complexes that his the wizard's treasures.

History
By the late 14 century DR, it was widely believed that Askalath's tower was once a pirate stronghold. However, Elminster Aumar stated that it was home of Askalath, whose corrupted name eventually became the island's name.

## Asheera

Asheera was an elf paladin from the world of Azallah who went on a quest to find and stop Morag of the Old Ones. After entering the Source Stone, in the demiplane there, she met the Hero of Neverwinter, who was on the same mission, in the .

Description
In the view of the Hero, she was identical in appearance and voice to Aribeth de Tylmarande. However, she was wearing different clothes.

Personality
Her behaviors seemed very similar to Aribeth's before the latter was corrupted. She was motivated by vengeance and a need to protect her home. Asheera also showed great determination towards fulfilling her mission. She was honorable to the point that no one seemed to question it. Like Aribeth she was also trusting towards the Hero of Neverwinter at the moment of their meeting. She and Aribeth both spoke in a nearly identical manner.

Possessions
Asheera's garb appeared alien to the Hero of Neverwinter. She possessed a magic amulet, which she got from magic-users of her world, to aid the struggle against Morag and her army of Old Ones. The enchanted item increased the wearer's resistance towards electricity, fire, magic, and attacks of reptilians.

 Activities
Asheera was part of the Shining Order. Before or during the invasion of her city, she successfully resisted Morag's temptations to help her with invading Sahaladar. Because her homeworld, Azallah, was under attack by Morag's forces, Asheera and others were sent into the Source Stone to kill the Old One queen and stop the invasion. However, they were defeated and she was the only survivor of the expedition. While fighting a losing battle against the Old Ones, Asheera was saved by the Hero of Neverwinter and told them about her homeworld and the war. The magic keeping her in the demiplane was weakening, and the time of Asheera's leaving the plane was coming closer, so the woman offered the Hero her amulet to support their efforts in finishing Morag off and finally ending the war against the Old Ones. It was unknown if Asheera avoided a confrontation with the Hero or peacefully left for Azallah.

History
Not much is known about Asheera before she entered the Source Stone. At some point she became a knight of the Shining Order. Just like Aribeth, Morag attemted to recruit her via haunting dreams that promised power. In contrast to Aribeth, she was able to succesfully resist those temptations. Her story suggests, that she was considered a competent enough paladin in her homeworld, to join the mission to stop Morag's forces.

 Appendix
Appearances

 Trivia

While she looks identical to Aribeth de Tylmarande both in her portrait and according to the Hero of Neverwinter, the game Neverwinter Nights classifies her as an elf, while Aribeth is a half-elf.
The names of the sound files with dialogue spoken by Asheera begin with vs_naribetf.
Asheera would not be able to level up as a paladin, because of her alignment. Gaining paladin levels is only possible for Lawful Good characters in Neverwinter Nights. This means she either changed her alignment during paladin training or the requirements for developing as a paladin are different in her world.

## Astral Prism

The Astral Prism, also referred to as the weapon or mysterious artifact, was the name given to a powerful gith device that appeared in the Realms during the late 15 century DR.

Description
It appeared as a spiked, black iron icosahedron inscribed with glyphs of the Gith language.

According to a githyanki disk found within the Prism itself, it was of infernal design and was part of a deal made between Vlaakith I and a devil.

Powers

The Astral Prism functioned as both a prison and a navigation device, leading to a pocket plane within the Astral Plane, where Vlaakith CLVII imprisoned the Prince of the Comet, Orpheus, the son of the original githyanki leader Gith herself. In addition, the artifact was able to project a field that disrupted illithid psionics due to the imprisoned Orpheus and his powers, freeing them from the Absolute's influence, making it a significant existential threat to the Grand Design and the Absolute's ambitions.

History

In or before the, the Church of Shar heard whispers of rivals, particularly the Cult of the Absolute. In the process of gathering information, they learned of an artifact vital to countering the Absolute's rising influence and highly feared by it, and learned of the Astral Prism's whereabouts by torturing a githyanki. The Sharran priestess Shadowheart was sent by her superiors on a mission to retrieve the item and return it to Baldur's Gate immediately, and she successfully recovered it.

Around the same time, mind flayer known as the Emperor was sent aboard a regrown Nautiloid to retrieve the Astral Prism, successfully locating it before others.It should be noted that the Emperor only claims that he was the first to find it. Contact with the Prism gave him a newfound sense of free will, allowing him to resist the telepathic control of the Elder Brain and break free.

Shadowheart was waylaid, however, when she was taken captive aboard a mind flayer nautiloid, implanted with a modified illithid tadpole, and forced to endure a trip across the planes. After traveling through Avernus and the Shadowfell, the nautiloid returned to the Prime Material plane, and crashed on the continent of Faerûn on Toril, fortunately mere miles away from Baldur's Gate.

Thereafter, the artifact was sought after by various groups, including the githyanki Kith'rak Voss, on behalf of Queen Vlaakith CLVII to retrieve it, and the three chosen of Cult of the Absolute.

## Ashes Plague

The Ashes Plague was a disease outbreak that struck the Shoon Imperium in the .

Effects
Afflicted individuals' skin turned a gray color and they suffered from seizures, blindness, and bleeding. Those who died from the disease died of the bleeding.

History
The plague largely struck Keltar, Manshaka, and Volothamp. As much as 9% of the population in these areas died, although many of them did not succumb to the disease but were slain in a vain attempt to prevent the spread of the plague.

The city of Calimport was spared an outbreak of the plague, although it still lost 0.2% of its population because of citizens contracting the illness while travelling.

Description
Aszloakh's moniker was given to him by inhabitants of the Gate because the saurial always wore a hooded weathercloak when outside on the city streets. He had a deep and rumbling voice and had many years of service to the Church of Finder Wyvernspur under his belt. Personality-wise, Aszloakh always remained patient and calm, even in the face of danger. The finhead saw humans from a friendly, humorous, yet cynical lens. He expected to be lied to and betrayed.

## Ason

Ason was pickpocket brother of Ginia the courtesan from the isle of Brynnlaw in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
During his time on the island, Ason was a child with chestnut hair.

History
Ason and his sister Ginia were raised in Brynnlaw since birth. Their mother died very early on, whilst their father was a tailor. Early on, Ginia was offered employment at the Spellhold asylum. After Brynnlaw fell under the control of Desharik, Ason's father was sentenced to death for breaking the law set out by the pirate king. Desharik told the newly orphaned Ginia that she had to pay for her father's crime, as his death wasn't enough. She was given to Chremy, his lieutenant as a reward for his service, who sent her to work as a courtesan against her will. If she refused, Chremy threatened to slaughter Ason, who, in turn, was forced to work for the pirate as a pickpocket on Brynnlaw's streets.

It was rumored that Ginia and her brother were eventually smuggled out of the island by Calahan with help from Abdel Adrian who sought entrance to Spellhold in the . Calahan agreed to take the youths to the village of Ulgoth's Beard, not far from Baldur's Gate, for a fee, where they would be raised by a woman named Therella.

History
In the, the Zhentarim mage Ghaubhan Szaurr put in motion a plan to join his blood with that of Shandril Shessair in an attempt to gain her spellfire powers, an act strongly implied to be carnal in nature. ## Aster Merris

Aster Merris was the regional leader of the Forgotten Lords, a major prosperous trading cartel active across the Sword Coast in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Aster was a young and slender woman with green eyes and shoulder-length chestnut hair. She carried herself with a curt and business-like demeanor. Aster's golden touch almost always guaranteed profit. She prided herself on the Forgotten Lords' honesty.

Relationships
The Church of Waukeen respected Aster Merris. She was considered to be a canny and resourceful merchant favored by Waukeen. Aster greatly distrusted Shadow Thieves of Amn, especially Belueth the Calm, a mercenary who might've joined merchant Sa'Sani's operations on the Sword Coast North. She also distrusted the Fated Winds mercantile firm that was rumored to had been associated with the Zhentarim. Aster did not openly accuse the competitors of anything. However, Aster stressed her mercantile company's honesty when dealing with new costers interested in joining the Lords.

History
By the, the Forgotten Lords were already an influential mercantile group that spread its dealings across the Sword Coast. After the Shadow War finished, the trade and economy were left crippled in the area surrounding Neverwinter. The Forgotten Lords were among three major trade cartels granted offices within the Temple of Waukeen in the city's Tyr's Eye district. The Church of Waukeen guided the revival of trade and refilling of its drained coffers of the northern city with the blessing of Lord Nasher Alagondar. Aster Merris, the Forgotten Lords' regional leader, was reassigned from the headquarters in Waterdeep to Neverwinter to supervise the newly established business.

During that period of time, a new coster joined the economy of the north. Sa'Sani led her Mercantile House to the Sword Coast and opened its headquarters in the Crossroad Keep. Two of her associates did business in Neverwinter, including interactions with the Forgotten Lords. Il'foss and Kizu disappeared soon after, secretly slain by the yuan-ti Forktongue cultists of an interloper deity, Zehir. Sa'Sani's hirelings interviewed Aster regarding the missing merchants and were given an opportunity to ally the Mercantile House with the Forgotten Lords.

## Ash wheat

Ash wheat was a species of wild wheat found in the Duskwood of the  Border Kingdoms.

Ash wheat's lifecycle affected the cycles of other animal species in the region. Ash wheat grew in abundance every spring, providing a generous food source for the badger mice population and swelling their numbers. The growing population of badger mice led to abundant food for puff adders, increasing the snake population.

## Askell Stockhold

Description
Dawnmaster Stockhold's stained glass depiction showed a tiefling with dark red skin, a pious look, and Lathanderian garb. The depiction was shown holding a holy symbol of Lathander with his raised right hand.

History
Dawnmaster Stockhold was one of four high-ranking members of the clergy who served at Rosymorn Monastery sometime before the . The other three were Seed, Welkinglory, and Vaseid. All four were depicted on a circular stained glass mosaic in an upper central chamber of the monastery. Around the mosaic were four pedestals, upon which laid each of their ceremonial weapons: masterfully crafted enchanted +1 items that were among those great works that attracted pilgrims from across Faerûn, Lathanderian and members of other faiths alike.

Dawnmaster Stockhold's grave could be found on a precipice near the southeast corner of Rosymorn Monastery. A ceremonial mace was buried with him.

## Astalagan

Astalagan was a bronze dragon who was mentioned in the legends about Stormwreck Isle.

History
Astalagan was one of the three great metallic dragons who defeated the great red dragon Sharruth long before the late 1400s DR. Legends say that he, the gold dragon Clyssavar, and the brass dragon Turadaer joined forces to battle Sharruth and confined her beneath the ocean floor.

It is unknown how exactly Astalagan died, but it was centuries prior to the late 1400s DR. A cloister called Dragon's Rest was built on the site of his death in the cliffs of Stormwreck Isle and it was protected by the remaining draconic magic. In the late 15 century, Dragon's Rest's elder was Runara, a bronze dragon disguised in human form and Astalagan's daughter. The dragon statue near the monastic cells was dedicated to Astalagan.

In the, a blue dragon wyrmling named Sparkrender had taken up residence in the Clifftop Observatory on Stormwreck Isle and attempted to harness the power of the island's fallen dragons. For this cause, he ordered his kobold followers Mek and Minn to build and paint sculptures of the five great dragons, Astalagan among them. When a group of adventurers and a bronze wyrmling named Aidron attempted to stop Sparkrender's ritual, the effigy of Astalagan gave them 'Astalagan's Blessing', restoring their health and surrounding them with warmth.

Description
Asparagus was an aging human woman with wispy gray hair, dressed in a simple dress with a corset.

History
In the, an army assembled by the Cult of the Absolute moved from the Moonrise Towers in Chionthar Valley to menace the city of Baldur's Gate, displacing countless refugees in the process along the way. Asparagus Mhallie fled to the nearby city of Baldur's Gate with her heard of dairy goats – her girls, her family. Sadly, her girls were taken – confiscated by the Flaming Fist mercenaries upon entry to be slaughtered due to ration shortage in the city. Despite being robbed of her livelihood and beloved beasts, Asparagus was granted entrance to the Lower City of Baldur's Gate, where she was protesting the confiscations along with other farmers near the Basilisk Gate.

## Astral diamond

Astral diamonds (abbreviated "ad") were tiny, glowing crystals that were used as currency on planes of existence with commercial economies in the World Axis cosmology. On Toril, they were regarded as valuable gemstones.

Description
Astral diamonds were clear, faceted gemstones that glowed with a faint silver light. As currency, a standard astral diamond weighed 1/500 of a pound (0.9 grams)—a tenth as much as a gold piece—and was worth the equivalent of 10,000 gp. They were often traded in sets of 5 or 10 linked together in silver or mithral settings, and were used for large transactions in markets across both the Astral Sea and the Elemental Chaos, including in Sigil and the City of Brass.

Astral diamonds could also be used as decorative elements (e.g., as jewelry or set into items), as spell components, and to craft lenses.

History
Astral diamonds occurred within astral dominions, and in particular, they tended to be found in dominions that were inhabited and lively. Mining operations attracted great interest, and abandoned mines often became home to deadly occupants. The richest veins were said to be in places like Mount Celestia. Rough astral diamonds had to be refined before they could be used as currency.

The Nar were known to craft steel "death masks" studded with astral diamonds.

Xorvintrothian artificers created devices that used lenses made of astral diamond. These lenses were tempered in the chaotic energies of Limbo, resulting in an ability to not only focus light but to filter undesirable energies. They were used to charge and to disable Far Realm batteries.

As of the late 15 century DR, astral diamond had been used to create the Fang Light, a magical and eternal beacon that helped to guide ships through the reefs and shoals off the west coast of Aglarond.

Notable Owners
 Amaunator, god of the sun, once possessed an oaken staff with a base inlaid with astral diamonds and topped with a massive astral diamond in the shape of a sun.
 Zhengyi the Witch-King kept a collection of smaller astral diamonds in a secret treasure compartment behind his throne.
