## Asmuth

Lord-prince Asmuth was a nobleman from Zhentil Keep in the mid–13 century DR. His younger brother Manshoon eliminated him to secure his position.

Personality
The depraved man was ruthless, like his brother. He was however far less subtle.

Abilities
He was a swordsman. Of note, he continued to have brigandly adventures, even slaying some adventurers from ambush, after being blinded.

Activities
While in the Keep, Asmuth liked destructive roistering and playing cruel pranks.

Relationships
Asmuth was the son of Harlshoon and the brother of Manshoon; being the child of a man with a seat on the Council granted him the unofficial position of "Lord-prince". Notably, his father was known to abuse his position of power over the whole family.

History
While in Zhentil Keep, Asmuth spent much time with his brother, Manshoon, and their friend Chess, playing cruel pranks and just being a pain. At some point, the three youths were sent out with gold and swords to prove themselves out in the world, following the custom of Zhent nobility. Each of them had a separate mission, in addition to that. Unknown to them, the mage Calkontor, Chess's father, intended to kill Harlshoon while they were out. He carried this out, but died in the process. Their parents' seats were instead occupied by Theilon Greencloak and Ulsan Baneservant three months after their departure, in the .

The trio sought out a barrow, which was purported to hold an enchanted sword amongst other things by the lore they had consulted. In this barrow, they came across a group of Harper women, who were after the same sword. As they were at cross-purposes, they crossed swords; during combat, Asmuth was blinded, and Manshoon lost a hand to the sword when he tried to snatch it. The two brothers were rescued by their friend Chess. Asmuth's blindness required his horse to be led by another. They wandered for a time, until one night they ambushed a band of adventurers. The three Zhents were victorious, and amongst their spoils were an enchanted wand and a spellbook, both of which Manshoon took. After a few more adventures as brigands, their trip home began.

On the way back, the trio learned of the deaths of their fathers while in a tavern, somewhere in the Dalelands, and resolved to inherit their seats on the council. Though at first Manshoon had thought to support his older brother, he believed that the older sibling would necessarily lead them into a protracted battle by trying to dominate Zhentil Keep with the strongest possible force, and chose another way. Manshoon resolved to dispose of Asmuth before they reached Zhentil Keep and arranged his fatal fall from a bridge over a deep gorge in the . This ensured his ability to take the seat on the council would not be challenged.

Rumors & Legends
It was popular knowledge, albeit false, in Zhentil Keep that Manshoon had been unable to save Asmuth, rather than actively causing his death.

## Astrala

Astrala, sometimes spelled Astralla, was a human waitress who served tables at the Golden Goblet Inn in Mossbridges in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Circa the, Astrala was a tall young woman with long brown hair. She had an average appearance, somewhere between being attractive and plain.

Relationships
She was familiar with a good half-orc fighter who occasionally visited the Goblet. The retired fighter, Chester Hearth, was notable to her because he paid with gems, instead of coins and was often accompanied by his guardian war dog named Suzzane.

History
The town of Mossbridges began growing after The Three Women War in the Vast. A new popular inn opened in the late 14 century DR, the Golden Goblet Inn, where Astrala found employment. She cheerfully greeted guests and offered them a table or a spot at the bar, where the inn's owner, Georgio, served drinks.

## Ashen Rock

Ashen Rock was a male tabaxi scout who resided in the Malar's Throat slums of Port Nyanzaru, Chult, during the late 15 century DR. He was a member of Port Nyanzaru's Citizens' Brigade and worked to defend the residents of Malar's Throat from the dangers of the jungle.

Personality
He was observant, independent, and gracious. He was unafraid to engage in combat but he preferred stealth, and so he liked to operate on his own.

Abilities
Ashen Rock was dexterous, stealthy, and had keen hearing and eyesight. He had good survival skills, and in combat, he favored his natural claws.

Possessions
He carried a potion of poison resistance.

History
During the events of the death curse in the late 15 century DR, Ashen Rock was a member of a Citizens' Brigade rescue party that embarked into the jungle when a group of pterafolk attacked Malar's Throat and abducted several residents. Ashen Rock became separated from the group and captured by the pterafolk, whom he observed to be erratic and diseased—possibly afflicted by the death curse—and to be sacrificing people to plant monsters in the jungle. He too was offered as a sacrifice alongside another prisoner, Siela. Although badly injured and trapped in brambles, he fought to escape from the twig and needle blights to which he had been offered, but his only hope for survival lay with adventurers who were also investigating the pterafolk.

## A Study of the Slayer

A Study of the Slayer was a tome of arcane research on the topic of the Bhaalspawn and the Slayer of Bhaal, penned by Jon Irenicus in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Contents
Regarding the Slayer book logged the research, observations, and musings of archmage Jon Irenicus in his quest to capture Abdel Adrian's piece of divinity. Irenicus claimed that even Bhaalspawn who did not follow their divine progenitor's bloody calling, like Gorion's Ward did, the evil taint was inescapable. The Slayer was locked inside each of the Bhaalspawn, awoken by strong feelings of divine rage and bloodlust, unachievable by simple mortals. This meeting of hatred pleased the deity. When a Bhaalspawn transformed into the Slayer, Bhaal's own avatar, their mind became shattered. Irenicus discovered that in his attempts to extract the essence from multiple Bhaalspawn. In his observation, the archmage also noted that Abdel Adrian, the Bhaalspawn of Candlekeep, might have been the Lord of Murder's favorite seed, pointing Irenicus towards his goal – capturing Gorion's Ward and extracting his divine essence.

History
A Study of the Slayer was a unique book penned sometime circa the  by Jon Irenicus's hand. By the, the book ended up in the hands of Volothamp Geddarm, who, in turn, loaned it to the heroes known as the Absolute Adversaries, when they faced with Orin the Red, a shapeshifting Bhaalspawn. However, the writer cordially asked the heroes to keep A Study of the Slayer away from bloodbaths due to the book's being one of a kind.

## Astarma White

Astarma White was a variety of white wine produced by Fendlara Astarma's family and exported out of the city of Urmlaspyr in Sembia in the late 14 century DR.

History
In the late 14 century DR, a shipment of Astarma White was brought to the Vast by Fendlara Astarma, on the behest of her father, who hoped she would develop the love for his lifelong passion of winemaking. Astarma vineyards produced good quality reds, whites, and rosés. Astarma White had a notably balanced fruity taste. Fendlara offered Astarma White for a single gold piece per hand-keg – an unbeatable price for such quality. Astarma win was entered into the wine-tasting competition during the year-end celebration of the  in Mossbridges, but it was unknown if it won. ## Ash tree

Ash trees were seemingly sentient trees that attacked travelers in the Burning Woods of the Shadowfell.

Description
These trees appeared blackened and glowing with crackling, ever-burning fire. They were further suffused with harmful necromantic energy.

These trees were firmly rooted to the ground and could not be harmed by any fire-, necrotic-, or psychic-based magic, nor did poisons affect them. However, it was possible to temporarily stun an ash tree by causing it to become magically cold.

Behavior
They behaved as if sentient and angry, lashing out at any creature that came within range of their  flaming branches. Anything ensnared by the tree was pulled back toward the burning trunk.

Ecology
Far from simply undead and burning trees, new ash trees could sprout as burning saplings and grow into full trees. As such, they could potentially be found not only throughout the Burning Wood, but inside the city of Evernight.

History
Ash trees first appeared when the Burning Woods were created during the events of the Ruining in the : when Mount Hotenow erupted on the Material Plane, its Shadowfell twin did as well, and never stopped.

## Assassin's headwrap

Assassin's headwrap was a unique enchanted thief's cowl from the city of Athkatla in Amn, created before the late 15 century DR.

Powers
Assassin's headwrap dramatically increased the wearer's ability to stealth and avoid detection. Additionally, its magic concealed the wearer after a successful killing strike.

History
Assassin's headwrap, originally part of a set of light armor, was commissioned by an unnamed Merchant Prince of Amn for his daughter, who sought to become an infamous assassin after dreaming about it. Unbeknownst to her father, the daughter began training to become an assassin with her father's less-than-savory contacts in Amn's criminal underbelly. Despite the daughter's fears, her Merchant Prince father, did not disavow her upon finding out. Instead, he commissioned the creation of assassin's headwrap and the rest of the stealth-enhancing magic armor for her.

Sometime later, by the late 15 century DR, assassin's headwrap was damaged and ripped apart. It could have been repaired by a master smith form Luskan, using a scarf and cloth of a hood, found in the Underdark in the Savage Frontier.

## Astral stalker

Astral stalkers were creatures of the Astral Plane that hunted across the planes.

Description
An astral stalker looked like a muscular humanoid, standing around 6 feet (1.8 meters) tall, with black-green skin, a prominent jaw, and great razor-sharp claws.

Personality
Astral stalkers lived for the thrill of hunting down prey, and gained status in their communities by the amount and strength of those creatures they hunted down. Thus they preferred intelligent prey as it would fight back far smarter than unintelligent beasts. Indeed they gained a great deal of pleasure from hunting down their most elusive prey, and would even make sure their quarry was somehow warned about their coming hunt in advance.

Always seeking to test themselves, they would hunt down stronger prey, but neither stupid nor reckless they would seldomly engage in the true hunt without extensive preparation, including scouting out the terrain and their opponents. Once the hunt was on, they would use ambush attacks.

Abilities
An astral stalker could shoot a poisoned dart from its throat, and turn invisible until the very moment it attacked their quarry. Once they had the identity of a prey creature, they had an uncanny ability to know where it was, even across planar boundaries.

Combat
In combat, the astral stalker would weaken their quarry with darts and terror before moving in and raking the prey with their claws. If the target escaped or shook off the effects of the poison, they would try to disappear before moving in once more.

Society
They gathered in small tribes led by the astral stalker with the most impressive hunting trophies, thus the leadership position could and would change hands whenever one of its members succeeded in hunting down new and mighty prey.

The astral stalker community had a list of Rules of the Hunt that they saw as absolute. First, if a quarry managed to escape and the astral stalker lost track of it, it would never again be hunted by that astral stalker. An employer who had hired an astral stalker was off-limits from becoming prey for at least one year after hiring the astral stalker. Finally, no astral stalker would ever hunt down another astral stalker.

History
They were created as living weapons of the gods in the Dawn War. After the Dawn War, the astral stalkers escaped to the Astral Sea.

## Assassin imp

Assassin imps were a specialized variety of imp from the Nine Hells. As per the name, they were among the deadliest of the imp varieties, being specially practiced in the murder of the helpless.

Description
Assassin imps resembled their common kin, ranging from  tall, but had scales ranging in hue from slate gray to jet-black as opposed to the standard red. They had a pair of bat-like wings and long, slender scorpion-like tails that dangled behind them in constant motion. Their mouths were filled with wickedly sharp, ivory-white teeth and a long, raspy tongue.

Personality
Clever, deceptive and murderous, assassin imps delighted in slaughtering defenseless innocents even moreso than most imps. Such was their wicked glee in spilling blood that they happily pursued any target they were given, but they particularly enjoyed killing those already pledged to their infernal master. They enjoyed painfully and bloodily awakening victims, only to render them unconscious a moment later.

Abilities
Assassin imps were fast flyers, resistant to fire, and had numerous spell-like abilities. These included powers such as invisibility, detect magic, and find traps at will, knock or cause light wounds thrice per day, and command once per day.

Ironically, the tail poison of assassin imps was not nearly as dangerous as that of their normal counterparts. Though harmful, the virulent venom was not especially lethal. Targets were at the very least slowed and perhaps drowsy, or they were were likely to be immobilized or could fall asleep with a sustained sting. At its most potent, they might fall into a deep, days-long state of hibernation, like that caused by the feign death spell.

There was a contrast to be drawn between the regular assassin imps and those known as "murder lords", whose powers differed from their lessers in their capacity for subterfuge. Firstly, rather than just turning invisible, they also became insubstantial and phased through objects, and after striking from invisibility they could teleport a short distance away.

Combat
Aided by their spell-like abilities like invisibility, assassin imps often struck without warning, avoiding detection until their victim drew near and diving at them. Foes caught unaware generally received longer injections of venom, while waking targets they attempted to put to sleep. Only when targets proved resistant to their poison did they fight properly, with a combination of tail stings and sharp blades. Some carried tiny daggers or jagged bits of scrap metal, most used straight razors, but all were adept at bleeding out the helpless.

Assassin imps often worked in teams, or as the vanguard of a proper assault. Generally they tried to kill their victims in some way linked to their professions, such as with objects related to their work. Otherwise, when they placed them in comas, they often lingered nearby in hopes of seeing their mistaken companions bury them alive, arranging for the truth to be revealed only after it was too late.

Society
Assassin imps were notably dedicated creatures, even to mortal masters. However, they would ruthlessly kill any being they deemed a threat to their master, even regardless of the master's own desire, often acting on their own to "defend" their lord. This could drive an assassin imp to attempt to murder anyone their master knew, loved ones included, if they believed the subject knew too much, and could ultimately draw unwanted attention.

Assassin imps were often sent to eliminate enemies of diabolists and devil cults.

Assassin imps spoke Common and Supernal, but could also communicate with others of their kind using a language some described as "purely evil in sound and expression".

In Ravenloft, assassin imps were the tools of the Dark Powers, and acted to drive the already evil into actions that would trap them in the Domains of Dread forever.

Ecology
Assassin imps had keen eyesight, augmented with  infravision. They were carnivorous creatures that operated at night.

Asta styled her long white hair back in a heavy ponytail and small crown, and wore a daring ensemble of a collar-and-midriff-baring bustenhalt with sabatons and a spiderleg-themed flair that framed her from behind.

## Assassin's dice

Assassin's dice was a set of two enchanted mythic dice, used as a unique sort of ranged weapon by assassins of the Underdark.

Description
Assassin's dice were carved out of a green substance with a skull carving on one of their sides. The dice radiated a green magical glow.

Powers
These wondrous items were used as weapons, thrown at the assassins' enemies. The dice thrown determined the target's fate upon collision: dramatically reduced ability to land a successful attack, reduced chance of landing powerful lucky blows, crippled ability to avoid attacks, and removed ability to deflect blows.

The dice's powers were boosted if used as a set that included the assassin's choker and assassin's belt, boosting the assassin's chances of landing lucky deadly strikes and their chances of avoiding incoming attacks.

## Asta the Deep-Minded

Asta the Deep-Minded was an elder dwarf woman who followed Hal Stoutheart's Tethyamar expedition in the 1480s DR.

Description
Asta had dark eyes and a bob of white hair. She wore a fine dress and carried an ornate staff.

Personality

In the best tradition of dwarven elders, Asta was suspicious, grumpy, and stubborn, and used this to help her kin. She immediately knew that Hal Stoutheart's promises were suspect, and remained unaffected by the influence of the jewels of ardor even as the expedition degenerated into chaos.

History

Asta arrived at the Tethyamar Mining Outpost when her own nephew Freygar Plumbline joined Hal Stoutheart's expedition. She immediately picked up how odd the situation was and how her fellow dwarves were turning into manic oddballs, and strongly suspected that something foul was afoot despite the general air of wealth and glory. When a group of opportunistic surfacers arrived at the outpost to get work, she roped them into helping the other dwarves stay alive and investigating the odd goings-on. Her benevolent scheme came to the aid of Freygar, Eluf the Steadfast, Hagen Silverthumb, Gunhild the Watchful, and finally Inger Ingersdottir as they ran into problems. Her suspicions were ultimately proven correct when it turned out that Hal Stoutheart was long-dead, and the expedition leader was none other than the demon lord Graz'zt, seeking the lure the dwarves to ruin by exploiting their own greed.

## Ashenwight

An ashenwight was a form of undead that arose from the bodies of humanoids who had died in areas corrupted by the Far Realm whilst consumed by feelings of cruelty and rage. If specifically arisen near a crystal vein infused with aberrant energies, or if one lingered in an area of fell magic, the ashenwight could potentially develop psionic abilities.

Description
These undead had corporeal bodies, with desiccated skin and glowing eyes.

Personality
Most had feral minds, retaining a only fragment of their former selves that was devoid of memories or thoughts. Such ashenwights were consumed by a compulsive desire to destroy any living creature they encountered.

Psionic ashenwights had exhibited exceptionally far greater sapience than the standard variety, though whatever consciousness they possessed was something wholly new and unrelated to whoever they were in life.

Abilities
Ashenwights typically fought by producing shards imbued with necrotic energy.

In addition to being able to produce more potent shards, psionic ashenwights could produce psionic effects similar to calm emotions and mage hand (without a visible hand). They could communicate telepathically. And they had the ability to manifest a subduing crown of jagged, spectral crystals around the head of a nearby creature, making them sluggish and attacking their psyche.

Society
While feral minded ashenwights lacked the sentience to seek anything more than destruction, the psionic variety were typically dedicated to either uncovering their past self or enlightening other ashenwights to their psionic potential.
Languages
Ashenwights knew the same languages that their body had spoken in life, though were unable to speak. Those psionically gifted were able to get around this with their telepathy.

History
Centuries prior to, mind flayers wrested control of Zorzula's Rest, an ancient duergar mining outpost beneath the Sword Mountains. During their takeover the illithids trapped the soul of Zorzula's last mining captain, Hjoldak Hollowhelm, within a canopic jar sealed shut by an intricate puzzle lock.

In 1491 DR, a group of adventurers traveling in the depths of Zorzula's Rest to uncover an illithid plot encountered several feral and psionic ashenwights that had arisen from the bodies of the outpost's former duergar denizens. They later came across Hjoldak's canopic jar in the private quarters of Ruxithid the Chosen. Through some means or another managed to free Hjoldak's soul from the jar, manifesting a psionic ashenwight that somehow retained Hjoldak's memories of the outpost's layout and hidden rooms. Eager to compensate his rescuers, the ashenwight offered to act as their guide through Zorzula's Rest. Later at the Underdark crossroads known as Gibbet's Crossing, in one had once been a duergar workshop, the adventurers encountered four more feral ashenwights.

Months prior, a pair of duergar mages that were disciples of Ilvaash brought four duergar warriors to the Brinzy Maze, the godlet's domain within the Far Realm, with the intention of sacrificing them. However, a fight broke out an all involved died. The two mages would arise from the dead as psionic ashenwights and go on to ally with a pair of phase spiders for mutual protection. These ashenwights would later be encountered and fought by the adventurers of Phandalin. The pair were crossed with Ilvaash, feeling it had betrayed them, but were unwilling to help the adventurers in working against it.

## Aszhgruzz

Aszhgruzz was a male alguduir, mate of Hizs, one of the two "swamp dragons" who made a home in a swamp and ended lives of several people from a nearby village in the 14 century DR.

History
Aszhgruzz and Hizs made a nest in a swamp, planning to raise their young within the boggy lands that were abundant with food. Aszhgruzz and his mate were quite intelligent and required quite a lot of meat to survive, which led them to hunt most living creatures near the nest, including some unlucky villagers. The couple learned to use various swamp beasts to weaken the larger prey before the alguduir were able to swoop from the skies and finish the job. Swamp rohches were among the creatures used by the alguduir to hunt or to defend themselves against adventurers. And adventurers were sure to come. Some were hired by a nearby alchemist-wizard who wanted one of both of the alguduir captured for their spell-reflecting abilities. Others were contracted by the villages after clawed bodies of their kin were discovered near the swamp. Other heroes might've been lured into the swamp by their enemies, hoping that the alguduir would hunt and kill the new prey.

## Astral rift

Astral rift was a conjuration spell from the 3 circle of magic, unique to githyanki and githzerai of the Astral Plane.

Effects
When astral rift was cast, it ripped open the planes, creating a gate that temporarily connected the Prime Material plane with the Astral. The gate allowed githyanki and githzerai reinforcements to arrive at Prime and return back with deadly swiftness. The caster had to put all their efforts into maintaining their astral rift, rendering them unable to move or do anything apart from concentrating on the gate or closing it.

History
Circa the, the warring factions of githyanki and githzerai used the astral rift spell the assault the adventuring band known as the Absolute Adversaries and the illithid calling itself the Emperor.

## Astral projection (psionic)

Astral projection was a psionic power of the psychoportation discipline that allowed a manifester to project themselves and a number of companions into the Astral Plane for the purposes of traveling to other planes of existence. In some respects it was similar to a cleric's astral spell and the power probability travel.

Effects

Unlike probability travel, which physically transported a manifester into the Astral Plane, astral projection created an astral form, a translucent, white projection of their own body and all possessions on their person, leaving the physical body behind in a state of suspended animation. And unlike both the astral spell and probability travel, a manifester could only transport themselves into the Astral Plane, though in the late 14 century DR this distinction was found to have disappeared. When transporting others, the manifester and their passengers had to be linked together in a circle at the time of manifestation. Passengers had to remain with the manifester at all times, lest they risk being stranded on the Astral Plane. The power took a total of 30 minutes to manifest, but had an indefinite duration, terminating whenever the manifester desired. Negate psionics being manifested upon either the physical body or the astral form would terminate the power.

This astral form was solid on the Astral Plane and behaved in most situations as though it were a physical body, but its actions could only effect other things in the astral. Much like the astral form created by the astral spell, this form was connected to the manifester's physical body by a silver cord, which was visible for the first  that stretched out from the astral body before merging with the plane, fading out into an invisible and intangible state

When an astral body finally reached a destination outside of the Astral Plane with a color pool, a temporary physical body was formed. This resembled the manifester's original body down to the exact detail, including physical and mental abilities, though lacked any of their physical possessions except for magic items and items under a spell effect, and was still tethered to the physical body by the silver cord. Due to the principle of "planar dissonance", it was impossible for a manifester to enter and form a temporary body on the same plane of existence as their physical body, not even by entering through a color pool, nor could they direct magical or psionic powers into that plane. The version of astral projection used in the late 14 century DR seemed to lack these restrictions.

If either this second body or the astral form were to be destroyed, the silver cord and the manifester's consciousness would return to their physical body, reviving it from its state of suspended animation. There were no lasting consequences to this, although reports existed of subsequent wariness and severe headaches in the worst cases.

If the nearly indestructible silver cord was somehow severed, the manifester would be simultaneously killed both astrally and materially, with the astral form left to dissolve into the Astral Plane itself. This death was irrevocable, only possible to undo through powerful means such as the wish spell.

For older versions of astral projection, a manifestation backfiring had the consequence of attracting the attention of some powerful entity within the Astral Plane.

Displays
This power exhibited no display when manifested.

Significance
The Dimensional anchor spell and its psionic equivalent could prevent a manifester from using astral projection.

Users
This power was knowable to psionicists and wild talents. It was particularly common as a wild talent among necromancers, whom were known for developing unusual mental abilities.

Astral projection was exhibited as an innate psionic ability by baku, ancient or older brainstealer dragons, elder brains, dhours, some githzerai, psionic illithids, adult intellect devourers, opinicus, and shedu.

Powerful shamans of the Great Worm tribe, who worshiped Uthgar's Great Worm beast totem, were capable of assuming a "great worm" form that conferred a number of psionic abilities, including astral projection.

Known/Notable Users
 Elrem the Wise, a powerful Uthgar shaman of the Great Worm tribe.The North: Guide to the Savage Frontier and Powers & Pantheons both state that the "great worm" form an Uthgar shaman can shapechange into confers a number of psionic powers. The powers listed are nearly the same as those that were originally detailed for Elrem back in The Savage Frontier, thus it can be inferred that Elrem's psionic powers are not unique to him.
 Lertis the Seer, a Banite high priest and psionic wild talent, who used astral projection to search that plane for Bane.
 K'yorl Odran, a drow psionicist and the matron mother of House Oblodra.
 Lugribossk, a proxy of the illithid god Ilsensine.

Appendix
Background
When psionics were updated for 3.5 edition in Expanded Psionics Handbook, the designers opted not to carry over astral projection, instead replacing it with two new powers, astral caravan and astral traveler.

Nansi Gretta sold the book in her Lower City shop, The Bibliophile; a copy could be found in the library of The Lodge, the Baldur's Gate chapter of the Society of Brilliance; and it was found in the Crimson Draughts potionmaker's shop.

## Askepel

Lord Askepel was a leader of the Knights of Synnoria on the island of Gwynneth in the Moonshae Isles during the late 15 century DR. He was a key participant in a Synnorian plot to overthrow the ruling leShay of Sarifal.

Personality

Askepel was old even by elven standards, and had lived so long as to have become a shell of his younger self: he'd lost nearly all of his ability to feel passion beyond pride and self-interest; he felt little kindness, love, or sense of friendship; and he had little interest in art or music.

He was coldly intelligent but arrogant and supremely assured of the superiority of his people, so much so that it made him incapable of understanding what motivated them. He assumed that other races simply did not share the morals of the Llewyrr, and he believed that the likes of humans should see it as an honor to be able to work alongside his Knights regardless of their needs or grievances. He reacted to any disagreement from humans as an insult, or as if he had smelled something foul, and made no effort to conceal his contempt for those he perceived as lesser. Any offense committed against the Llewyrr was a grave and severe act in his eyes.

Relationships
He had a cousin (his father's sister's son) named Oemeril Talos–claere, who served as a guardian of the borders of Synnoria.

History
Askepel and his fellow conspirators against the leShay operated out of an abandoned fortress known as Harrowfast that sat on the border of Synnoria. His dissatisfaction with his rulers had grown beginning in the early 15 century DR as it became clear to him that the leShay could no longer be trusted.

In the late 1470s DR, a fellow member of the Knights named Mindarion successfully brought fomorians and humans into a discussion of alliance to overthrow the leShay. The Knights of Synnoria lacked the numbers to win a war against the leShay's supporters and drow mercenaries, and so this alliance was necessary for them to succeed in their desire to be free of High Lady Ordalf and to replace her with her half-Llewyrr sister, Amaranth. However, the savvy Mindarion was badly injured just prior to the meeting, leaving Askepel to negotiate in his place. Askepel seemed unable or unwilling to acknowledge the fomorians and humans as true allies, instead expecting them to unquestioningly serve his interests. Negotiations deteriorated slowly, they collapsed after the human leader, Captain Rurik, and a companion of the fomorians, Suka, accidentally trespassed into Synnoria and killed three Llewyrr while escaping, including Oemeril Talos–claere. After Rurik fled from Harrowfast, Askepel and his Knights confronted the fomorian princess, Marabaldia, and attempted to take Suka into custody. In response, Marabaldia invoked her "evil eye" to stun him, and despite his self-assurance that her powers would not work on him, he was struck completely immobile. Askepel survived the ensuing battle—although he lost his sword—while Suka and the fomorians fled from Harrowfast with the help of Mindarion.

## Astral searcher

Astral searchers were mindless creatures of nebulous humanoid shape whose only driving action was to invade the material plane and possess a body, thereby becoming sentient.

Creation
Astral searchers were created from concentrated or traumatic thoughts, such as combat or destructive spells cast in the astral plane.

Behavior
Astral searchers wandered the astral plane searching for weak points between the planes so as to invade the prime material plane. Driven by their past connections to the prime material they obsessively searched for humanoid bodies to possess. Should they manage to arrive in other planes inhabited by humanoids, they would hunt down the nearest individual and attack its psyche. Once the psyche of their victim was destroyed they could then inhabit the body and become a new sapient creature.

As newly created sapient creatures, they would exhibit the emotion that created them in the first place, such as rage, determination, fear, and so on. As they had not yet learned to speak they could only make noises reflecting their emotional state.

Combat
Astral searchers attacked with ghostly claws that tore at the psyche. Purely mental, no ordinary defense or armor could stand against their attacks.

Ecology
Until they possessed a humanoid, they had no capability for thought. Once in possession of a body they would assume that the emotion that created it was the natural state of the world, and thus sought to recreate the atmosphere around its own creation. They were however very quick learners, and within a few hours of possession abilities such as language or non-weapon-related proficiencies would come back to the new inhabitant. Only memories and life experience were gone for good, as if the individual suffered from permanent amnesia. Of the original personality, it was gone forever and only by a true wish spell could it be brought back.

## Astil

Astil adh Saida al Manshaka was a human fighter and a founder and leader of the Clenched Fist, a Rundeen-affiliated mercenary and adventuring company based in Schamedar in Calimshan in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Abilities
He had a natural talent for tactics and strategy, which served him well in battle. He was also strong.

History
Astil was once a slave of Saida al Manshaka in the city of Manshaka. He killed her in the 1340s DR, but chose to keep his slave name.

To keep his freedom he joined the Clenched Fist in Schamedar, then a rowdy gang of mercenaries before it grew into a full-fledged mercenary company.

## Asp

Asps were small venomous snakes often encountered in the warmer parts of Toril.

Description
They were small snakes around  in length with a very dangerous venom, able to kill those bitten within a minute.

Ecology
Habitats
Asps were found nearly everywhere in the Chultan jungles, thriving in the warmth and humidity of that land. They were also found in the Badu al-Kabîr desert in Zakhara.

History
In the, in the cursed city of Medinat Muskawoon, the Temple of Ra's courtyard was protected by a throng of asps, preventing anyone from simply strolling into the temple proper.

## Astarion's Book of Hungers

Astarion's Book of Hungers is a digital-only supplement for the Forgotten Realms using the 5-edition (revised)  ruleset published by Wizards of the Coast. It is set to be released in November 2025 alongside Forgotten Realms: Heroes of Faerûn and Forgotten Realms: Adventures in Faerûn. It covers vampires and Baldur's Gate III tie-ins. It is included in the Forgotten Realms Ultimate Bundle on D&D Beyond along with Netheril's Fall and Lorwyn: First Light.
